◆ has_jacobian()

virtual bool casadi::Callback::has_jacobian ( ) const
virtual

Override has_jacobian() to return true and get_jacobian() to return a Function with inputs [all nominal inputs, all nominal outputs] and one output per (output, input) pair, each the dense or sparse Jacobian block of that output with respect to that input (row-major over the pairs). Use the inames, onames and opts passed in: they carry the expected names and the "derivative_of" option. The returned Function may be an SX/MX Function (then higher derivatives are exact) or another Callback (keep a reference to it).

>>> class F43(Callback):
...   def __init__(self, name, opts={}):
...     Callback.__init__(self)
...     self.construct(name, opts)
...   def get_sparsity_in(self, i): return Sparsity.dense(4, 1)
...   def get_sparsity_out(self, i): return Sparsity.dense(3, 1)
...   def eval(self, arg):
...     a, b, c, d = vertsplit(arg[0])
...     return [vertcat(sin(c)*d+d**2, 2*a+c, b**2+5*c)]
...   def has_jacobian(self): return True
...   def get_jacobian(self, name, inames, onames, opts):
...     print(name, inames, onames)
...     x = SX.sym("x", 4)
...     y = SX.sym("y", Sparsity(3, 1))
...     a, b, c, d = vertsplit(x)
...     J = blockcat([[0, 0, d*cos(c), sin(c)+2*d], [2, 0, 1, 0], [0, 2*b, 5, 0]])
...     return Function(name, [x, y], [J], inames, onames, opts)
>>> f = F43("f")
>>> x = MX.sym("x", 4)
>>> J = Function("J", [x], [jacobian(f(x), x)])
jac_f ['i0', 'out_o0'] ['jac_o0_i0']
>>> J([1, 2, 0, 3])
DM(
[[0, 0, 3, 6],
 [2, 0, 1, 0],
 [0, 4, 5, 0]])


>> c = {'classdef F43 < casadi.Callback', 'methods'};
>> c = [c, {'function self = F43(name)'}];
>> c = [c, {'self@casadi.Callback(); construct(self, name);', 'end'}];
>> c = [c, {'function sp = get_sparsity_in(self, i)'}];
>> c = [c, {'sp = casadi.Sparsity.dense(4, 1);', 'end'}];
>> c = [c, {'function sp = get_sparsity_out(self, i)'}];
>> c = [c, {'sp = casadi.Sparsity.dense(3, 1);', 'end'}];
>> c = [c, {'function r = eval(self, arg)'}];
>> c = [c, {'v = arg{1}; a = v(1); b = v(2); c = v(3); d = v(4);'}];
>> c = [c, {'r = {[sin(c)*d+d^2; 2*a+c; b^2+5*c]};', 'end'}];
>> c = [c, {'function b = has_jacobian(self)', 'b = true;', 'end'}];
>> c = [c, {'function J = get_jacobian(self, name, inames, onames, opts)'}];
>> c = [c, {'disp(name); disp(inames); disp(onames);'}];
>> c = [c, {'x = casadi.SX.sym(''x'', 4); a = x(1); b = x(2); c = x(3); d = x(4);'}];
>> c = [c, {'y = casadi.SX.sym(''y'', casadi.Sparsity(3, 1));'}];
>> c = [c, {'Jm = [0, 0, d*cos(c), sin(c)+2*d; 2, 0, 1, 0; 0, 2*b, 5, 0];'}];
>> c = [c, {'J = casadi.Function(name, {x, y}, {Jm}, inames, onames, opts);'}];
>> c = [c, {'end', 'end', 'end'}];
>> d = tempname; mkdir(d); addpath(d); fid = fopen(fullfile(d, 'F43.m'), 'w');
>> fprintf(fid, '%s\n', c{:}); fclose(fid);
>> f = F43('f'); x = MX.sym('x', 4); J = Function('J', {x}, {jacobian(f(x), x)});

i0

>> J([1; 2; 0; 3])

 [2, 0, 1, 0],
 [0, 4, 5, 0]]
>> rmpath(d); rmdir(d, 's');

◆ has_reverse()

virtual bool casadi::Callback::has_reverse ( casadi_int  nadj) const
virtual

Override has_reverse(nadj) to return true for the numbers of directions you support and get_reverse() to return a Function with inputs [nominal inputs, nominal outputs, one adjoint seed per output] and one adjoint sensitivity per input. For nadj>1 the seeds and sensitivities are horizontally stacked. Reverse mode is what gradient-based NLP solvers use for the objective gradient. reverse(nadj) caches the result.

>>> class Sq(Callback):
...   def __init__(self, name, opts={}):
...     Callback.__init__(self)
...     self.construct(name, opts)
...   def eval(self, arg):
...     return [arg[0]**2]
...   def has_reverse(self, nadj): return nadj == 1
...   def get_reverse(self, nadj, name, inames, onames, opts):
...     print(nadj, name, inames, onames)
...     x, y, yb = SX.sym("x"), SX.sym("y", Sparsity(1, 1)), SX.sym("yb")
...     return Function(name, [x, y, yb], [2*x*yb], inames, onames, opts)
>>> f = Sq("f")
>>> x = MX.sym("x")
>>> G = Function("G", [x], [gradient(f(x), x)])
1 adj1_f ['i0', 'out_o0', 'adj_o0'] ['adj_i0']
>>> G(3)
DM(6)


>> c = {'classdef SqAdj < casadi.Callback', 'methods'};
>> c = [c, {'function self = SqAdj(name)'}];
>> c = [c, {'self@casadi.Callback(); construct(self, name);', 'end'}];
>> c = [c, {'function r = eval(self, arg)', 'r = {arg{1}^2};', 'end'}];
>> c = [c, {'function b = has_reverse(self, nadj)', 'b = nadj == 1;', 'end'}];
>> c = [c, {'function F = get_reverse(self, nadj, name, inames, onames, opts)'}];
>> c = [c, {'disp(nadj); disp(name); disp(inames); disp(onames);'}];
>> c = [c, {'x = casadi.SX.sym(''x''); y = casadi.SX.sym(''y'', casadi.Sparsity(1, 1));'}];
>> c = [c, {'yb = casadi.SX.sym(''yb'');'}];
>> c = [c, {'F = casadi.Function(name, {x, y, yb}, {2*x*yb}, inames, onames, opts);'}];
>> c = [c, {'end', 'end', 'end'}];
>> d = tempname; mkdir(d); addpath(d); fid = fopen(fullfile(d, 'SqAdj.m'), 'w');
>> fprintf(fid, '%s\n', c{:}); fclose(fid);
>> f = SqAdj('f'); x = MX.sym('x'); G = Function('G', {x}, {gradient(f(x), x)});
1
adj1_f
i0
out_o0
adj_o0
adj_i0
>> G(3)
6
>> rmpath(d); rmdir(d, 's');


julia> struct Sq <: ca.Callback end
julia> ca.eval_(::Sq, arg) = [arg[1]^2]
julia> ca.has_reverse(::Sq, nadj) = nadj == 1
julia> function ca.get_reverse(::Sq, nadj, name, inames, onames, opts)
           println(nadj, " ", name, " ", inames, " ", onames)
           x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y", ca.Sparsity(1, 1)); yb = ca.SX.sym("yb")
           ca.Function(name, [x, y, yb], [2x*yb], inames, onames, opts)
       end
julia> f = ca.callback(Sq(), "f");
julia> x = ca.MX.sym("x");
julia> G = ca.Function("G", [x], [ca.gradient(f(x), x)]);
1 adj1_f ["i0", "out_o0", "adj_o0"] ["adj_i0"]
julia> G(3)
DM(6)

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_oj

◆ if_else()

static Function casadi::Function::if_else ( const std::string &  name,
const Function &  f_true,
const Function &  f_false,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
)
staticinherited

a condition input

The result takes the condition as first input followed by the inputs of the branches; a nonzero condition (truncated to integer) selects f_true, zero selects f_false. Only the selected branch is evaluated numerically. The signature is taken from f_false (see conditional).

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function.if_else("f", Function("t", [x], [x**2]), Function("e", [x], [x]))
>>> f
Function(f:(i0,i1)->(o0) Switch)
>>> f(1, 3), f(0, 3), f(5, 3)
(DM(9), DM(3), DM(9))


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f_true = Function('t', {x}, {x^2}); f_false = Function('e', {x}, {x});
>> f = Function.if_else('f', f_true, f_false); disp(f)
f:(i0,i1)->(o0) Switch
>> [f(1, 3), f(0, 3), f(5, 3)]
[[9, 3, 9]]


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f_true = ca.Function("t", [x], [x^2]); f_false = ca.Function("e", [x], [x]);

◆ init()

virtual void casadi::Callback::init ( )
inlinevirtual

Called from construct() after the inputs and outputs are known and the options are processed, before finalize(). Override to allocate data; the default does nothing.

>>> class Sq(Callback):
...   def __init__(self, name, opts={}):
...     Callback.__init__(self)
...     print("before construct")
...     self.construct(name, opts)
...     print("after construct")
...   def init(self): print("init", self.n_in())
...   def finalize(self): print("finalize")
...   def eval(self, arg):
...     return [arg[0]**2]
>>> f = Sq("f")
before construct
init 1
finalize
after construct


>> c = {'classdef SqInit < casadi.Callback', 'methods'};
>> c = [c, {'function self = SqInit(name)'}];

>> c = [c, {'function finalize(self)', 'disp(''finalize'');', 'end'}];

◆ is_a()

bool casadi::Function::is_a ( const std::string &  type,
bool  recursive = true 
) const
inherited

type is a class name as printed after the signature: "SXFunction", "MXFunction", "Map", "Switch", "External", "Nlpsol", "Conic", "Integrator", "Rootfinder" and the plugin names (e.g. "Ipopt"). With recursive true (default) base classes match as well ("Nlpsol" for an Ipopt solver, "Map" for a ThreadMap, "FunctionInternal" for everything).

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("x")], [SX(0)])
>>> f.is_a("SXFunction"), f.is_a("MXFunction"), f.class_name()
(True, False, 'SXFunction')
>>> f.map(2, "thread").is_a("Map"), f.map(2, "thread").is_a("Map", False)
(True, False)


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('x')}, {SX(0)});
>> fprintf('%d %d %s\n', f.is_a('SXFunction'), f.is_a('MXFunction'), f.class_name())
1 0 SXFunction
>> F = f.map(2, 'thread'); fprintf('%d %d\n', F.is_a('Map'), F.is_a('Map', false))
1 0

◆ is_diff_in() [1/2]

std::vector<bool> casadi::Function::is_diff_in ( ) const
inherited

All true by default; set with options "is_diff_in" and "is_diff_out". A non-differentiable input or output is skipped by jacobian, forward, reverse and factory: the corresponding derivative blocks are all-zero sparse. The signature printout marks them with a '#'. Without argument, the list for all inputs or outputs is returned.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); n = SX.sym("n")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, n], [x*n], ["x", "n"], ["r"], {"is_diff_in": [True, False]})
>>> f
Function(f:(x,#n)->(r) SXFunction)
>>> f.is_diff_in(), f.is_diff_in(1), f.is_diff_out()
([True, False], False, [True])
>>> f.jacobian()
Function(jac_f:(x,#n,out_r[1x1,0nz])->(jac_r_x,#jac_r_n[1x1,0nz]) SXFunction)


>> x = SX.sym('x'); n = SX.sym('n');
>> opts = struct('is_diff_in', [true, false]);
>> f = Function('f', {x, n}, {x*n}, {'x', 'n'}, {'r'}, opts); disp(f)
f:(x,#n)->(r) SXFunction
>> f.is_diff_in(), f.is_diff_in(1), f.is_diff_out()
1 0
0
1
>> f.jacobian()
jac_f:(x,#n,out_r[1x1,0nz])->(jac_r_x,#jac_r_n[1x1,0nz]) SXFunction


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); n = ca.SX.sym("n");
julia> opts = Dict("is_diff_in" => [true, false]);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, n], [x*n], ["x", "n"], ["r"], opts);
julia> f
Function(f:(x,#n)->(r) SXFunction)
julia> ca.is_diff_in(f), ca.is_diff_in(f, 1), ca.is_diff_out(f)
(Bool[1, 0], false, Bool[1])
julia> ca.jacobian(f)
Function(jac_f:(x,#n,out_r[1x1,0nz])->(jac_r_x,#jac_r_n[1x1,0nz]) SXFunction)

See also

◆ is_diff_out() [1/2]

◆ is_diff_out() [2/2]

bool casadi::Function::is_diff_out ( casadi_int  ind) const
inherited

All true by default; set with options "is_diff_in" and "is_diff_out". A non-differentiable input or output is skipped by jacobian, forward, reverse and factory: the corresponding derivative blocks are all-zero sparse. The signature printout marks them with a '#'. Without argument, the list for all inputs or outputs is returned.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); n = SX.sym("n")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, n], [x*n], ["x", "n"], ["r"], {"is_diff_in": [True, False]})
>>> f
Function(f:(x,#n)->(r) SXFunction)
>>> f.is_diff_in(), f.is_diff_in(1), f.is_diff_out()
([True, False], False, [True])
>>> f.jacobian()
Function(jac_f:(x,#n,out_r[1x1,0nz])->(jac_r_x,#jac_r_n[1x1,0nz]) SXFunction)

◆ jac_sparsity() [1/2]

const std::vector<Sparsity>& casadi::Function::jac_sparsity ( bool  compact = false) const
inherited

One pattern per (output, input) pair, ordered by output first, as in jacobian. Patterns are computed by sparsity propagation the first time and cached.

Parameters
compactWhen true, the pattern is over the nonzeros of output and input (nnz_out-by-nnz_in) rather than over all elements (numel_out-by-numel_in)

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); D = SX.sym("D", Sparsity.diag(2))
>>> f = Function("f", [x, D], [x*D, sin(x)])
>>> f.jac_sparsity()
[Sparsity(4x1,2nz), Sparsity(4x4,2nz), Sparsity(1x1), Sparsity(1x4,0nz)]
>>> f.jac_sparsity(True)
[Sparsity(2x1), Sparsity(2x2,2nz), Sparsity(1x1), Sparsity(1x2,0nz)]


>> x = SX.sym('x'); D = SX.sym('D', Sparsity.diag(2));
>> f = Function('f', {x, D}, {x*D, sin(x)});
>> sp = f.jac_sparsity(); disp(numel(sp)), cellfun(@disp, sp)
4
4x1,2nz
4x4,2nz
1x1
1x4,0nz
>> cellfun(@disp, f.jac_sparsity(true))
2x1
2x2,2nz
1x1
1x2,0nz

◆ jacobian()

Function casadi::Function::jacobian ( ) const
inherited

The result is named jac_<name> and takes all inputs followed by all outputs (named out_<output>); the output values are only read when uses_output() is true and may be passed as 0 otherwise. It returns one block per (output, input) pair, ordered by output first: for f:(x,y)->(r,s) the outputs are jac_r_x, jac_r_y, jac_s_x, jac_s_y. Blocks involving a non-differentiable input or output are all-zero sparse. Each block is a size_out(o)-times-size_in(i) matrix where the output and input are vectorized column-major; blocks that are structurally zero have an all-zero sparsity pattern.

The result is cached: calling jacobian again returns the same object as long as it is alive. The derivative itself is built by symbolic AD for SX and MX functions, by the solver's own sensitivity method for solver objects, or raises "Derivatives cannot be calculated" for classes without any derivative support. For a single block, factory("...", ..., ["jac:r:x"]) avoids computing the others.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"])
>>> J = f.jacobian()
>>> print(J)
jac_f:(x,y[2],out_r[2x1,0nz],out_s[1x1,0nz])->(jac_r_x[2],jac_r_y[2x2,2nz],...) SXFunction
>>> J(1, [2, 3], 0, 0)
(DM([2, 3]), DM(
[[1, 00],
 [00, 1]]), DM(0.540302), DM([[00, 00]]))
>>> f.jacobian().name_out()
['jac_r_x', 'jac_r_y', 'jac_s_x', 'jac_s_y']


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y', 2);
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y, sin(x)}, {'x', 'y'}, {'r', 's'});
>> J = f.jacobian(); disp(J)
jac_f:(x,y[2],out_r[2x1,0nz],out_s[1x1,0nz])->(jac_r_x[2],jac_r_y[2x2,2nz],...) SXFunction
>> [jac_r_x, jac_r_y, jac_s_x, jac_s_y] = J(1, [2; 3], 0, 0);
>> jac_r_x, jac_r_y, jac_s_x, jac_s_y
[2, 3]
[[1, 00],
 [00, 1]]
0.540302
[[00, 00]]
>> J.name_out()
jac_r_x
jac_r_y
jac_s_x
jac_s_y


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y", 2);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"]);
julia> J = ca.jacobian(f);
julia> ca.name(J), ca.n_in(J), ca.n_out(J)
("jac_f", 4, 4)
julia> jac_r_x, jac_r_y, jac_s_x, jac_s_y = J(1, [2, 3], 0, 0);
julia> jac_r_x, jac_s_x, jac_s_y
([2, 3], 0.540302, [[00, 00]])
julia> jac_r_y
DM(
[[1, 00],
 [00, 1]])
julia> ca.name_out(J)
4-element Vector{String}:
 "jac_r_x"
 "jac_r_y"
 "jac_s_x"
 "jac_s_y"

See also
factory, forward, reverse, jac_sparsity, uses_output

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1w6

◆ jit() [1/2]

static Function casadi::Function::jit ( const std::string &  name,
const std::string &  body,
const std::vector< std::string > &  name_in,
const std::vector< std::string > &  name_out,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
)
staticinherited

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1v2

Create a just-in-time compiled function from a C language string

body is the body of a C function that is compiled with the "compiler" plugin (option "compiler", default "shell" i.e. the system C compiler on most builds, or "clang") and loaded as an external function. This needs a working C compiler at run time; the source and library files are removed afterwards unless "jit_cleanup" is false.

Inside the body, the nonzeros of input k are available through a pointer named name_in[k] (type const casadi_real*) and the nonzeros of output k must be written through a pointer named name_out[k] (type casadi_real*), in column-major order of the sparsity pattern. casadi_real is double unless the code generator is told otherwise. By default (option "buffered": true) inputs and outputs are copied through a work vector, so the pointers are never null; with "buffered": false a null input means all-zero and a null output may be skipped. Write "return 1;" to signal an evaluation failure.

The result has no derivatives unless option "enable_fd": true (finite differences) is passed or a C body for the Jacobian is supplied with option "jac" (and "hess" for its Jacobian).

Parameters
nameFunction name
bodyC code of the function body, without the enclosing braces
name_inInput names, also the C variable names
name_outOutput names, also the C variable names
sparsity_inSparsity of each input; all scalar when omitted
sparsity_outSparsity of each output; all scalar when omitted
optsOptions; "compiler", "jit_options", "buffered", "jac", "hess" and the common Function options

Example (not run here because it invokes a compiler): f = Function.jit("f", "r[0] = x[0]*x[0] + y[0];", ["x", "y"], ["r"]) gives a function f:(x,y)->(r) that evaluates like any other Function.

See also
external

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1v3

◆ map() [1/5]

Function casadi::Function::map ( casadi_int  n,

inherited

◆ map() [2/5]

Function casadi::Function::map ( casadi_int  n,
const std::string &  parallelization = "serial" 
) const
inherited

For f:(a, p)->(s) the result is F:(A, P)->(S) with A = horzcat(a0, ..., a_(n-1)), the same for P, and S = horzcat(f(a0, p0), ..., f(a_(n-1), p_(n-1))). Every input and output is widened to n times its number of columns; when calling, a non-widened argument is repeated for all evaluations. The graph size and construction time are independent of n. For n == 1 the function itself is returned; n < 1 raises "Degenerate map operation".

Parameters
nNumber of evaluations
parallelizationHow the evaluations are run: "serial" (a Map node evaluated in a loop; cached, so repeated calls return the same object), "openmp" (OpenMP parallel loop, requires an OpenMP build), "thread" (std::thread per evaluation), "unroll" (n explicit calls in an MXFunction), "inline" (like unroll with the calls inlined). Other values raise an error.
max_num_threadsWith "thread" or "openmp": split the n evaluations into at most this many parallel chunks, each a serial map

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); p = SX.sym("p")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, p], [p*x**2], ["x", "p"], ["y"])
>>> F = f.map(3)
>>> F
Function(map3_f:(x[1x3],p[1x3])->(y[1x3]) Map)
>>> F(DM([[1, 2, 3]]), 2)
DM([[2, 8, 18]])
>>> f.map(4, "thread", 2)
Function(threadmap2_map2_f:(x[1x4],p[1x4])->(y[1x4]) ThreadMap)
>>> f.map(3, "unroll")
Function(f_3:(x[1x3],p[1x3])->(y[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> f.map(1) is f, f.map(1).name()
(False, 'f')


>> x = SX.sym('x'); p = SX.sym('p');
>> f = Function('f', {x, p}, {p*x^2}, {'x', 'p'}, {'y'});
>> F = f.map(3); disp(F)
map3_f:(x[1x3],p[1x3])->(y[1x3]) Map
>> F(DM([1, 2, 3]), 2)
[[2, 8, 18]]
>> f.map(4, 'thread', 2)
threadmap2_map2_f:(x[1x4],p[1x4])->(y[1x4]) ThreadMap
>> f.map(3, 'unroll')
f_3:(x[1x3],p[1x3])->(y[1x3]) MXFunction
>> f.map(1).name()
f


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); p = ca.SX.sym("p");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, p], [p*x^2], ["x", "p"], ["y"]);
julia> F = f.map(3);
julia> F
Function(map3_f:(x[1x3],p[1x3])->(y[1x3]) Map)
julia> F([1 2 3], 2)
DM([[2, 8, 18]])
julia> f.map(4, "thread", 2)
Function(threadmap2_map2_f:(x[1x4],p[1x4])->(y[1x4]) ThreadMap)
julia> f.map(3, "unroll")
Function(f_3:(x[1x3],p[1x3])->(y[1x3]) MXFunction)
julia> f.map(1) === f, ca.name(f.map(1))
(false, "f")

See also
mapaccum, mapsum, call

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1wj

◆ map() [3/5]

Function casadi::Function::map ( casadi_int  n,
const std::vector< bool > &  reduce_in,
const std::vector< bool > &  reduce_out = std::vector< bool >(),
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
) const
inherited

Inputs listed in reduce_in are not widened: the same value is used in all n evaluations. Outputs listed in reduce_out are summed over the evaluations instead of stacked. The index and name overloads build an MXFunction around a plain map (parallelization as in map; opts is currently ignored). The boolean-mask overload creates a dedicated serial MapSum node wrapped in an MXFunction built with opts; masks must have length n_in and n_out (an empty reduce_out means no reduction).

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); p = SX.sym("p")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, p], [p*x, x], ["x", "p"], ["y", "z"])
>>> F = f.map("F", "serial", 3, ["p"], ["y"])
>>> F
Function(F:(x[1x3],p)->(y,z[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> F(DM([[1, 2, 3]]), 2)
(DM(12), DM([[1, 2, 3]]))
>>> f.map(3, [False, True], [True, False])(DM([[1, 2, 3]]), 2)
(DM(12), DM([[1, 2, 3]]))


>> x = SX.sym('x'); p = SX.sym('p');
>> f = Function('f', {x, p}, {p*x, x}, {'x', 'p'}, {'y', 'z'});
>> F = f.map('F', 'serial', 3, {'p'}, {'y'}); disp(F)
F:(x[1x3],p)->(y,z[1x3]) MXFunction
>> [y, z] = F(DM([1, 2, 3]), 2); disp(y), disp(z)
12
[[1, 2, 3]]
>> G = f.map(3, [false, true], [true, false]);
>> [y, z] = G(DM([1, 2, 3]), 2); disp(y), disp(z)
12
[[1, 2, 3]]

Inputs listed in reduce_in are not widened: the same value is used in all n evaluations. Outputs listed in reduce_out are summed over the evaluations instead of stacked. The index and name overloads build an MXFunction around a plain map (parallelization as in map; opts is currently ignored). The boolean-mask overload creates a dedicated serial MapSum node wrapped in an MXFunction built with opts; masks must have length n_in and n_out (an empty reduce_out means no reduction).

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); p = SX.sym("p")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, p], [p*x, x], ["x", "p"], ["y", "z"])
>>> F = f.map("F", "serial", 3, ["p"], ["y"])
>>> F
Function(F:(x[1x3],p)->(y,z[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> F(DM([[1, 2, 3]]), 2)
(DM(12), DM([[1, 2, 3]]))
>>> f.map(3, [False, True], [True, False])(DM([[1, 2, 3]]), 2)
(DM(12), DM([[1, 2, 3]]))


>> x = SX.sym('x'); p = SX.sym('p');
>> f = Function('f', {x, p}, {p*x, x}, {'x', 'p'}, {'y', 'z'});
>> F = f.map('F', 'serial', 3, {'p'}, {'y'}); disp(F)
F:(x[1x3],p)->(y,z[1x3]) MXFunction
>> [y, z] = F(DM([1, 2, 3]), 2); disp(y), disp(z)
12
[[1, 2, 3]]
>> G = f.map(3, [false, true], [true, false]);
>> [y, z] = G(DM([1, 2, 3]), 2); disp(y), disp(z)
12
[[1, 2, 3]]


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); p = ca.SX.sym("p");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, p], [p*x, x], ["x", "p"], ["y", "z"]);
julia> F = f.map("F", "serial", 3, ["p"], ["y"]);
julia> F
Function(F:(x[1x3],p)->(y,z[1x3]) MXFunction)
julia> F([1 2 3], 2)
(12, [[1, 2, 3]])
julia> f.map(3, [false, true], [true, false])([1 2 3], 2)
(12, [[1, 2, 3]])

See also
map, mapsum

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1wk

◆ mapaccum() [1/5]

Function casadi::Function::mapaccum ( casadi_int  N,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
) const
inherited

as inputs

The first n_accum inputs are accumulators: in every step they take the value of the first n_accum outputs of the previous step. For a step f:(x, u)->(x_next, y) the result is F:(x0, U)->(X, Y) with U = horzcat(u0, ..., u_(N-1)), X = horzcat(x1, ..., x_N), Y = horzcat(y0, ..., y_(N-1)) and x_(k+1), y_k = f(x_k, u_k). Non-accumulated inputs and all outputs are stacked horizontally over the N steps; the accumulator inputs keep their original shape. An accumulator output may be wider than its input (e.g. 1-by-2 feeding a 1-by-1 input): only the last columns are fed back.

Compared with writing the loop in the host language the construction is much faster and the graph is a tower of nested unrolled loops of at most base iterations (option "base", default 10; -1 unrolls completely). This keeps memory use proportional to base rather than N at a small evaluation cost.

The overloads differ in how accumulators are selected: a count n_accum (default 1), or explicit index/name lists accum_in and accum_out of equal length that may pick arbitrary inputs and outputs. The unnamed overload names the result "mapaccum_" + name(). fold is mapaccum returning only the final accumulator state.

Parameters
nameName of the result
NNumber of steps, must be positive
n_accumNumber of leading inputs/outputs that are accumulated; at most min(n_in, n_out)
opts"base" plus options for the resulting MXFunction

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); u = SX.sym("u")
>>> step = Function("step", [x, u], [x + u, x*u], ["x", "u"], ["x_next", "y"])
>>> F = step.mapaccum(3)
>>> F
Function(mapaccum_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> F(1, DM([[1, 2, 3]]))
(DM([[2, 4, 7]]), DM([[1, 4, 12]]))
>>> step.mapaccum("F", 3, ["x"], ["x_next"])
Function(F:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> step.fold(3)
Function(fold_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next,y[1x3]) MXFunction)
>>> step.fold(3)(1, DM([[1, 2, 3]]))
(DM(7), DM([[1, 4, 12]]))
>>> try:
...   step.mapaccum(0)
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("mapaccum: N must be positive" in str(e))
True


>> x = SX.sym('x'); u = SX.sym('u');
>> step = Function('step', {x, u}, {x + u, x*u}, {'x', 'u'}, {'x_next', 'y'});
>> F = step.mapaccum(3); disp(F)
mapaccum_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction
>> [X, Y] = F(1, DM([1, 2, 3])); disp(X), disp(Y)
[[2, 4, 7]]
[[1, 4, 12]]
>> step.mapaccum('F', 3, {'x'}, {'x_next'})
F:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next[1x3],y[1x3]) MXFunction
>> G = step.fold(3); disp(G)
fold_step:(x,u[1x3])->(x_next,y[1x3]) MXFunction
>> [X, Y] = G(1, DM([1, 2, 3])); disp(X), disp(Y)
7
[[1, 4, 12]]
>> try, step.mapaccum(0); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'N must be positive')); end
1

(7, [[1, 4, 12]])
julia> try

       catch e
           println(occursin("mapaccum: N must be positive", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

julia> ca.fold(step, 3)(1, [1 2 3])
(7, [[1, 4, 12]])
julia> try
           ca.mapaccum(step, 0)
       catch e
           println(occursin("mapaccum: N must be positive", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

See also
map, fold

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1wi

◆ mapsum()

std::vector<MX> casadi::Function::mapsum ( const std::vector< MX > &  x,
const std::string &  parallelization = "serial" 
) const
inherited

Each argument is either the input itself or n copies stacked horizontally (N-by-(n*M) for an N-by-M input); the largest such n sets the number of evaluations and non-stacked arguments are shared across evaluations. Returns the sum over the n evaluations of each output, as MX expressions calling an internal map-and-reduce function.

Parameters
xList of n_in() MX arguments
parallelization"serial" only; other values raise an error

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, x - y], ["x", "y"], ["p", "d"])
>>> p, d = f.mapsum([MX.sym("a"), MX.sym("b", 1, 3)])
>>> p
MX(mapsum(a, b){0})
>>> f.mapsum([2, DM([[1, 2, 3]])])
[MX(mapsum(2, [[1, 2, 3]]){0}), MX(mapsum(2, [[1, 2, 3]]){1})]
>>> evalf(f.mapsum([2, DM([[1, 2, 3]])])[0])
DM(12)


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y');
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y, x - y}, {'x', 'y'}, {'p', 'd'});
>> res = f.mapsum({MX.sym('a'), MX.sym('b', 1, 3)}); disp(res{1})
mapsum(a, b){0}
>> res = f.mapsum({2, DM([1, 2, 3])}); disp(res{1}), disp(res{2})
mapsum(2, [[1, 2, 3]]){0}
mapsum(2, [[1, 2, 3]]){1}
>> evalf(res{1})
12


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, x - y], ["x", "y"], ["p", "d"]);
julia> p, d = ca.mapsum(f, [ca.MX.sym("a"), ca.MX.sym("b", 1, 3)]);
julia> p
MX(mapsum(a, b){0})
julia> ca.mapsum(f, [2, ca.DM([1 2 3])])
2-element Vector{MX}:
 MX(mapsum(2, [[1, 2, 3]]){0})
 MX(mapsum(2, [[1, 2, 3]]){1})
julia> ca.evalf(ca.mapsum(f, [2, ca.DM([1 2 3])])[1])
DM(12)

See also
map, call

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1wh

◆ mx_in() [1/3]

const std::vector<MX> casadi::Function::mx_in ( ) const
inherited

Convenient for calling the function symbolically or for building a wrapper: f(f.mx_in()) is an MX expression of the outputs. Each call creates new symbols; they are not the symbols the function was constructed from. All inputs (no argument), one by index, or one by name.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("a"), SX.sym("B", Sparsity.diag(2))], [SX(0)],
...              ["a", "B"], ["r"])
>>> f.sx_in()
[SX(a), SX(
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]])]
>>> f.mx_in(1), f.mx_in("a").shape
(MX(B), (1, 1))
>>> f.sx_out(), f.mx_out(0)
([SX(r)], MX(r))


>> a = SX.sym('a'); B = SX.sym('B', Sparsity.diag(2));
>> f = Function('f', {a, B}, {SX(0)}, {'a', 'B'}, {'r'});
>> in = f.sx_in(); disp(in{1}), disp(in{2})
a
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]]
>> f.mx_in(1), size(f.mx_in('a'))
B
1 1
>> out = f.sx_out(); disp(out{1}), disp(f.mx_out(0))
r
r


julia> a = ca.SX.sym("a"); B = ca.SX.sym("B", ca.Sparsity.diag(2));
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [a, B], [ca.SX(0)], ["a", "B"], ["r"]);
julia> a_in, B_in = ca.sx_in(f);
julia> a_in
SX(a)
julia> B_in
SX(
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]])
julia> ca.mx_in(f, 1), size(ca.mx_in(f, "a"))
(B, (1, 1))
julia> ca.sx_out(f), ca.mx_out(f, 0)
(SX[r], r)

See also
sx_out, mx_out, sparsity_in

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1x4

◆ n_instructions()

casadi_int casadi::Function::n_instructions ( ) const
inherited

The algorithm is the sequence of operations evaluated for a call, including the input and output instructions; instruction_id, instruction_input, instruction_output, instruction_constant and instruction_MX inspect them by index. Other classes raise.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y + 1])
>>> f.n_instructions()
6
>>> for k in range(f.n_instructions()):
...   print(f.instruction_id(k), f.instruction_input(k), f.instruction_output(k))
45 [0, 0] [0]
45 [1, 0] [1]
3 [0, 1] [0]
44 [] [1]
1 [0, 1] [0]
46 [0] [0, 0]
>>> f.instruction_id(3) == OP_CONST, f.instruction_constant(3)
(True, 1.0)


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y');
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y + 1});
>> f.n_instructions()
6
>> arrayfun(@(k) f.instruction_id(k), 0:5)
45 45 3 44 1 46
>> f.instruction_input(2), f.instruction_output(2)
0 1
0
>> f.instruction_id(3) == OP_CONST, f.instruction_constant(3)
1
1


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y + 1]);
julia> ca.n_instructions(f)
6
julia> for k in 0:ca.n_instructions(f)-1
           println(ca.instruction_id(f, k), " ", ca.instruction_input(f, k), " ",
                   ca.instruction_output(f, k))
       end
45 [0, 0] [0]
45 [1, 0] [1]
3 [0, 1] [0]
44 Int64[] [1]
1 [0, 1] [0]
46 [0] [0, 0]
julia> ca.instruction_id(f, 3) == ca.OP_CONST, ca.instruction_constant(f, 3)
(true, 1.0)

See also
instruction_id, instructions_sx, n_nodes

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1xe

◆ n_nodes()

casadi_int casadi::Function::n_nodes ( ) const
inherited

Counts the unique nodes of the output expressions, inputs included; a cheap measure of graph size for comparing transformations. Other classes raise.

>>> x = MX.sym("x")
>>> Function("f", [x], [sin(x) + sin(x)]).n_nodes()
4


>> x = MX.sym('x');
>> Function('f', {x}, {sin(x) + sin(x)}).n_nodes()
4


julia> x = ca.MX.sym("x");
julia> ca.n_nodes(ca.Function("f", [x], [sin(x) + sin(x)]))
4

See also

◆ name_in() [1/2]

const std::vector<std::string>& casadi::Function::name_in ( ) const
inherited

Names come from the constructor; when none were given they are i0, i1, ... Solver objects have fixed schemes, e.g. x0, p, lbx, ubx, lbg, ubg, lam_x0, lam_g0 for nlpsol.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y")
>>> Function("f", [x, y], [x+y]).name_in()
['i0', 'i1']
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x+y], ["x", "y"], ["r"])
>>> f.name_in(), f.name_in(1), f.name_out()
(['x', 'y'], 'y', ['r'])


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y');
>> Function('f', {x, y}, {x+y}).name_in()
i0
i1
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x+y}, {'x', 'y'}, {'r'});
>> f.name_in(), f.name_in(1), f.name_out()
x
y
y
r


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y");

 "i1"

(["x", "y"], "y", ["r"])

◆ nnz_in() [1/3]

casadi_int casadi::Function::nnz_in ( ) const
inherited

Without argument, the sum over all inputs. The nonzero count is what numerical evaluation reads for the input; it is smaller than numel_in for sparse inputs.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("x", 2), SX.sym("D", Sparsity.diag(3))], [SX(1)])
>>> f.nnz_in(0), f.nnz_in(1), f.nnz_in()
(2, 3, 5)
>>> f.numel_in(0), f.numel_in(1), f.numel_in()
(2, 9, 11)


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('x', 2), SX.sym('D', Sparsity.diag(3))}, {SX(1)});
>> fprintf('%d %d %d\n', f.nnz_in(0), f.nnz_in(1), f.nnz_in())
2 3 5
>> fprintf('%d %d %d\n', f.numel_in(0), f.numel_in(1), f.numel_in())
2 9 11


julia> D = ca.SX.sym("D", ca.Sparsity.diag(3));
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [ca.SX.sym("x", 2), D], [ca.SX(1)]);
julia> ca.nnz_in(f, 0), ca.nnz_in(f, 1), ca.nnz_in(f)
(2, 3, 5)
julia> ca.numel_in(f, 0), ca.numel_in(f, 1), ca.numel_in(f)
(2, 9, 11)

See also
nnz_out, numel_in, sparsity_in

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1vc

◆ nz_from_in()

std::vector<double> casadi::Function::nz_from_in ( const std::vector< DM > &  arg) const
inherited

nonzeros

nz_from_in flattens a list of n_in() DM arguments (shape rules as in call) into the concatenated nonzeros, in input order and column-major within each input; nz_to_in does the reverse, the list must have exactly nnz_in() entries. nz_from_out and nz_to_out do the same for the outputs with nnz_out(). This is the memory layout that generated C code and FunctionBuffer use.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("x"), SX.sym("D", Sparsity.diag(2))], [SX(0)])
>>> f.nz_from_in([1, DM([[2, 0], [0, 3]])])
[1.0, 2.0, 3.0]
>>> f.nz_to_in([1, 2, 3])
[DM(1), DM(
[[2, 00],
 [00, 3]])]
>>> try:
...   f.nz_to_in([1, 2])
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Dimension mismatch. Expecting 3, got 2" in str(e))
True


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('x'), SX.sym('D', Sparsity.diag(2))}, {SX(0)});
>> f.nz_from_in({1, DM([2, 0; 0, 3])})
1 2 3
>> arg = f.nz_to_in([1, 2, 3]); disp(arg{1}), disp(arg{2})
1
[[2, 00],
 [00, 3]]
>> try, f.nz_to_in([1, 2]); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'Expecting 3, got 2')); end
1


julia> D = ca.SX.sym("D", ca.Sparsity.diag(2));
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [ca.SX.sym("x"), D], [ca.SX(0)]);
julia> ca.nz_from_in(f, [1, ca.DM([2 0; 0 3])])
3-element Vector{Float64}:
 1.0
 2.0
 3.0
julia> x_in, D_in = ca.nz_to_in(f, [1, 2, 3]);
julia> x_in
DM(1)
julia> D_in
DM(
[[2, 00],
 [00, 3]])
julia> try
           ca.nz_to_in(f, [1, 2])
       catch e
           println(occursin("Dimension mismatch. Expecting 3, got 2", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

See also
convert_in, generate_in, FunctionBuffer

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1x6

◆ oracle()

Function casadi::Function::oracle ( ) const
inherited

For nlpsol, qpsol, rootfinder and integrator objects this is the Function with the problem expressions (e.g. nlp:(x,p)->(f,g)); for other classes an error is raised.

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> opts = {"qpsol": "qrqp", "print_header": False, "print_iteration": False,
...         "print_time": False, "qpsol_options": {"print_header": False}}
>>> S = nlpsol("S", "sqpmethod", {"x": x, "f": x**2}, opts)
>>> S.oracle()
Function(nlp:(x,p[])->(f,g[0]) SXFunction)
>>> try:
...   S.oracle().oracle()
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("'oracle' not defined for SXFunction" in str(e))
True


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> opts = struct('qpsol', 'qrqp', 'print_header', false, 'print_iteration', false);
>> opts.print_time = false; opts.qpsol_options = struct('print_header', false);
>> S = nlpsol('S', 'sqpmethod', struct('x', x, 'f', x^2), opts);
>> S.oracle()
nlp:(x,p[])->(f,g[0]) SXFunction
>> try, S.oracle().oracle(); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'not defined')); end
1


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> opts = Dict("qpsol" => "qrqp", "print_header" => false, "print_iteration" => false,
                   "print_time" => false, "qpsol_options" => Dict("print_header" => false));
julia> S = ca.nlpsol("S", "sqpmethod", Dict("x" => x, "f" => x^2), opts);
julia> ca.oracle(S)
Function(nlp:(x,p[])->(f,g[0]) SXFunction)
julia> try
           ca.oracle(ca.oracle(S))
       catch e
           println(occursin("'oracle' not defined for SXFunction", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

See also
get_function, find_functions

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1vu

◆ reverse()

Function casadi::Function::reverse ( casadi_int  nadj) const
inherited

The result is named adj<nadj>_<name> and has n_in + n_out + n_out inputs: the original inputs, the original outputs (out_<name>) and the adjoint seeds adj_<output>, each with nadj times the columns of the output. Its n_in outputs adj_<input> are the adjoint sensitivities, nadj stacked horizontally. Falls back to the full Jacobian when the class has no reverse mode; raises when it has no derivatives at all. Results are cached while alive. nadj must be positive.

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"])
>>> R = f.reverse(1)
>>> R
Function(adj1_f:(x,y[2],out_r[2x1,0nz],out_s[1x1,0nz],adj_r[2],adj_s)->(adj_x,adj_y[2])
SXFunction)
>>> R(1, [2, 3], 0, 0, [1, 1], 0)
(DM(5), DM([1, 1]))


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y', 2);
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y, sin(x)}, {'x', 'y'}, {'r', 's'});
>> R = f.reverse(1); disp(R)
adj1_f:(x,y[2],out_r[2x1,0nz],out_s[1x1,0nz],adj_r[2],adj_s)->(adj_x,adj_y[2]) SXFunction
>> [adj_x, adj_y] = R(1, [2; 3], 0, 0, [1; 1], 0); disp(adj_x), disp(adj_y)
5
[1, 1]


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y", 2);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y, sin(x)], ["x", "y"], ["r", "s"]);
julia> R = f.reverse(1);
julia> R
Function(adj1_f:(x,y[2],out_r[2x1,0nz],out_s[1x1,0nz],adj_r[2],adj_s)->(adj_x,adj_y[2]) SXFunction)
julia> R(1, [2, 3], 0, 0, [1, 1], 0)
(5, [1, 1])

See also
forward, jacobian, factory

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1wr

◆ save()

void casadi::Function::save ( const std::string &  fname,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
) const
inherited

Writes a serialization of the function to fname (conventionally with the extension .casadi). The file is portable across platforms and languages (Python, MATLAB, C++); it is compatible across CasADi versions as far as the serialization format allows.

Parameters
fnameFile name
optsSerializer options, e.g. "debug"

>>> import os
>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [x**2])
>>> f.save("f.casadi")
>>> g = Function.load("f.casadi")
>>> g, g(3)
(Function(f:(i0)->(o0) SXFunction), DM(9))
>>> os.remove("f.casadi")


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {x^2});
>> f.save('f.casadi'); g = Function.load('f.casadi'); disp(g), disp(g(3))
f:(i0)->(o0) SXFunction
9
>> delete('f.casadi');


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x], [x^2]);
julia> ca.save(f, "f.casadi")
julia> g = ca.Function.load("f.casadi");
julia> println(g); g(3)
f:(i0)->(o0) SXFunction
DM(9)
julia> rm("f.casadi")

See also
load, serialize, FileSerializer

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_240

◆ serialize()

std::string casadi::Function::serialize ( const Dict &  opts = Dict()) const
inherited

Any Function can be serialized, including solvers and functions with embedded calls; a just-in-time compiled function is stored according to option "jit_serialize". The string is binary-safe text; it is also what the Python pickle protocol and MATLAB save use.

Parameters
opts"debug" (bool) adds consistency markers to the stream

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [sin(x)], ["x"], ["y"])
>>> s = f.serialize()
>>> g = Function.deserialize(s)
>>> g, g(1)
(Function(f:(x)->(y) SXFunction), DM(0.841471))


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {sin(x)}, {'x'}, {'y'});
>> s = f.serialize(); g = Function.deserialize(s); disp(g), disp(g(1))
f:(x)->(y) SXFunction
0.841471


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x], [sin(x)], ["x"], ["y"]);
julia> s = ca.serialize(f);
julia> g = ca.Function.deserialize(s);
julia> println(g); g(1)
f:(x)->(y) SXFunction
DM(0.841471)

See also
deserialize, save, load

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1x2

◆ size1_in() [1/2]

casadi_int casadi::Function::size1_in ( casadi_int  ind) const
inherited

size1_in is the number of rows, size2_in the number of columns and size_in the pair (rows, columns). The input is selected by index or by name; an unknown name raises an error listing the available names, an index out of range raises an error.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("A", 2, 3), SX.sym("b", 2)], [SX(0)], ["A", "b"], ["c"])
>>> f.size_in(0), f.size1_in("A"), f.size2_in("A")
((2, 3), 2, 3)
>>> f.size_in("b")
(2, 1)
>>> try:
...   f.size_in("nope")
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("Available names are: [A, b]" in str(e))
True


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('A', 2, 3), SX.sym('b', 2)}, {SX(0)}, {'A', 'b'}, {'c'});
>> disp(f.size_in(0)), fprintf('%d %d\n', f.size1_in('A'), f.size2_in('A'))
2 3
2 3
>> f.size_in('b')
2 1
>> try, f.size_in('nope'); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'Available names')); end
1


julia> A = ca.SX.sym("A", 2, 3); b = ca.SX.sym("b", 2);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [A, b], [ca.SX(0)], ["A", "b"], ["c"]);
julia> ca.size_in(f, 0), ca.size1_in(f, "A"), ca.size2_in(f, "A")
((2, 3), 2, 3)
julia> ca.size_in(f, "b")
(2, 1)
julia> try
           ca.size_in(f, "nope")
       catch e
           println(occursin("Available names are: [A, b]", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

See also
size_out, sparsity_in, nnz_in, numel_in

Definition at line 916 of file function.hpp.

◆ slice()

Function casadi::Function::slice ( const std::string &  name,
const std::vector< casadi_int > &  order_in,
const std::vector< casadi_int > &  order_out,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
) const
inherited

The expressions are unchanged; only the lists of inputs and outputs are rearranged. Indices may be repeated or omitted. An omitted input that an output depends on makes the result have free variables (construction then fails unless "allow_free" is in opts). Classes other than SXFunction and MXFunction are wrapped first.

Parameters
nameName of the result
order_inIndices of the original inputs to keep, in the new order
order_outIndices of the original outputs to keep, in the new order
optsOptions for the new function

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y")
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x + y, x - y, 2*x], ["x", "y"], ["a", "b", "c"])
>>> g = f.slice("g", [1, 0], [2, 0])
>>> g, g(1, 2)
(Function(g:(y,x)->(c,a) SXFunction), (DM(4), DM(3)))
>>> f.slice("h", [0], [2])
Function(h:(x)->(c) SXFunction)


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y');
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x + y, x - y, 2*x}, {'x', 'y'}, {'a', 'b', 'c'});
>> g = f.slice('g', [1, 0], [2, 0]); disp(g)
g:(y,x)->(c,a) SXFunction
>> [c, a] = g(1, 2); disp(c), disp(a)
4
3
>> f.slice('h', 0, 2)
h:(x)->(c) SXFunction


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x + y, x - y, 2x], ["x", "y"], ["a", "b", "c"]);
julia> g = ca.slice(f, "g", [1, 0], [2, 0]);

◆ sparsity_in() [1/2]

const Sparsity& casadi::Function::sparsity_in ( casadi_int  ind) const
inherited

Numerical arguments are projected onto this pattern before evaluation: entries outside it are dropped silently. Symbolic inputs keep the pattern of the expression passed to the constructor.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("D", Sparsity.diag(2))], [SX(0)], ["D"], ["r"])
>>> f.sparsity_in(0)
Sparsity(2x2,2nz)
>>> f.sparsity_in("D").is_diag()
True
>>> f.sparsity_out(0)
Sparsity(1x1)


>> f = Function('f', {SX.sym('D', Sparsity.diag(2))}, {SX(0)}, {'D'}, {'r'});
>> f.sparsity_in(0)
2x2,2nz
>> f.sparsity_in('D').is_diag()
1
>> f.sparsity_out(0)
1x1


julia> D = ca.SX.sym("D", ca.Sparsity.diag(2));
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [D], [ca.SX(0)], ["D"], ["r"]);
julia> ca.sparsity_in(f, 0)
Sparsity(2x2,2nz)
julia> ca.is_diag(ca.sparsity_in(f, "D"))
true
julia> ca.sparsity_out(f, 0)
Sparsity(1x1)

See also

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1vr

◆ stats()

Dict casadi::Function::stats ( int  mem = 0) const
inherited

The content depends on the class: solvers report e.g. "success", "return_status", "iter_count"; with option "record_time" every function reports "t_wall_total", "t_proc_total" and "n_call_total". Raises an error when the function has not been evaluated numerically yet.

Parameters
memMemory object (from checkout) whose statistics to return; 0 is the memory used by ordinary calls

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [x**2], {"record_time": True})
>>> try:
...   f.stats()
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("first evaluate it numerically" in str(e))
True
>>> r = f(2)
>>> sorted(f.stats().keys())
['n_call_total', 't_proc_total', 't_wall_total']


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {x^2}, struct('record_time', true));
>> try, f.stats(); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'first evaluate it numerically')); end
1
>> r = f(2); disp(strjoin(sort(fieldnames(f.stats()))', ' '))
n_call_total t_proc_total t_wall_total


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x], [x^2], Dict("record_time" => true));
julia> try
           ca.stats(f)
       catch e
           println(occursin("first evaluate it numerically", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true
julia> r = f(2);
julia> sort(collect(keys(ca.stats(f))))
3-element Vector{String}:
 "n_call_total"
 "t_proc_total"
 "t_wall_total"

See also
checkout

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_2mj

◆ sx_in() [1/3]

const std::vector<SX> casadi::Function::sx_in ( ) const
inherited

Convenient for calling the function symbolically or for building a wrapper: f(f.mx_in()) is an MX expression of the outputs. Each call creates new symbols; they are not the symbols the function was constructed from. All inputs (no argument), one by index, or one by name.

>>> f = Function("f", [SX.sym("a"), SX.sym("B", Sparsity.diag(2))], [SX(0)],
...              ["a", "B"], ["r"])
>>> f.sx_in()
[SX(a), SX(
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]])]
>>> f.mx_in(1), f.mx_in("a").shape
(MX(B), (1, 1))
>>> f.sx_out(), f.mx_out(0)
([SX(r)], MX(r))


>> a = SX.sym('a'); B = SX.sym('B', Sparsity.diag(2));
>> f = Function('f', {a, B}, {SX(0)}, {'a', 'B'}, {'r'});
>> in = f.sx_in(); disp(in{1}), disp(in{2})
a
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]]
>> f.mx_in(1), size(f.mx_in('a'))
B
1 1
>> out = f.sx_out(); disp(out{1}), disp(f.mx_out(0))
r
r


julia> a = ca.SX.sym("a"); B = ca.SX.sym("B", ca.Sparsity.diag(2));
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [a, B], [ca.SX(0)], ["a", "B"], ["r"]);
julia> a_in, B_in = ca.sx_in(f);
julia> a_in
SX(a)
julia> B_in
SX(
[[B_0, 00],
 [00, B_1]])
julia> ca.mx_in(f, 1), size(ca.mx_in(f, "a"))
(B, (1, 1))
julia> ca.sx_out(f), ca.mx_out(f, 0)
(SX[r], r)

See also
sx_out, mx_out, sparsity_in

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1x4

◆ transform() [1/4]

Function casadi::Function::transform ( const Dict &  opts = Dict()) const
inherited

Only defined for SXFunction and MXFunction. The result has the same signature, names and options; only the expression graph differs.

The dict form runs a single "simplify" pass whose tasks are selected with boolean options, all defaulting to on except combine_terms:

  • empty_inputs: an input whose symbols do not occur in any output is replaced by an all-zero sparse matrix of the same shape (its nnz becomes 0)
  • cse: common subexpression elimination
  • ref_count: drop unreferenced nodes, repeated until a fixed point
  • const_folding: evaluate constant subexpressions
  • combine_terms: merge like terms of sums

Unknown keys raise an error.

The passes form runs an explicit list of passes in order. Each pass is a list whose first entry is the verb:

  • ["simplify", task, ...] with tasks from the list above; an integer before a task is its run count (N>0: N times, 0: until the graph stops shrinking)
  • ["expand"]: expand to an SXFunction
  • ["external", library, operation] or [..., opts]: a transform from a plugin library

Here opts accepts only "verbose"; passing the boolean task options raises an error. With "verbose": true the node and instruction counts after each pass are printed.

Parameters
fnameName of the result; defaults to the current name
passesExplicit list of passes
optsDict form: task booleans and "verbose"; passes form: "verbose" only

>>> z = MX.sym("z"); w = MX.sym("w")
>>> f = Function("f", [z, w], [sin(z)*cos(z) + sin(z)*cos(z)], ["z", "w"], ["r"])
>>> f, f.n_instructions()
(Function(f:(z,w)->(r) MXFunction), 9)
>>> g = f.transform()
>>> g, g.n_instructions()
(Function(f:(z,w[1x1,0nz])->(r) MXFunction), 6)
>>> f.transform([["simplify", "cse"]]).n_instructions()
6
>>> f.transform("fe", [["expand"]])
Function(fe:(z,w)->(r) SXFunction)
>>> try:
...   f.transform([["nope"]])
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("unknown pass verb" in str(e))
True


>> z = MX.sym('z'); w = MX.sym('w');
>> f = Function('f', {z, w}, {sin(z)*cos(z) + sin(z)*cos(z)}, {'z', 'w'}, {'r'});
>> f, f.n_instructions()
f:(z,w)->(r) MXFunction
9
>> g = f.transform(); disp(g), disp(g.n_instructions())
f:(z,w[1x1,0nz])->(r) MXFunction
6
>> f.transform({{'simplify', 'cse'}}).n_instructions()
6
>> f.transform('fe', {{'expand'}})
fe:(z,w)->(r) SXFunction
>> try, f.transform({{'nope'}}); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'unknown pass')); end
1


julia> z = ca.MX.sym("z"); w = ca.MX.sym("w");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [z, w], [sin(z)*cos(z) + sin(z)*cos(z)], ["z", "w"], ["r"]);
julia> println(f); ca.n_instructions(f)
f:(z,w)->(r) MXFunction
9
julia> g = ca.transform(f);
julia> println(g); ca.n_instructions(g)
f:(z,w[1x1,0nz])->(r) MXFunction
6
julia> ca.n_instructions(ca.transform(f, [["simplify", "cse"]]))
6
julia> ca.transform(f, "fe", [["expand"]])
Function(fe:(z,w)->(r) SXFunction)
julia> try
           ca.transform(f, [["nope"]])
       catch e
           println(occursin("unknown pass verb", sprint(showerror, e)))
       end
true

See also
expand

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_2ig

◆ transform() [3/4]

  • empty_inputs: an input whose symbols do not occur in any output is replaced by an all-zero sparse matrix of the same shape (its nnz becomes 0)
  • cse: common subexpression elimination
  • ref_count: drop unreferenced nodes, repeated until a fixed point
  • const_folding: evaluate constant subexpressions
  • combine_terms: merge like terms of sums

Unknown keys raise an error.

The passes form runs an explicit list of passes in order. Each pass is a list whose first entry is the verb:

  • ["simplify", task, ...] with tasks from the list above; an integer before a task is its run count (N>0: N times, 0: until the graph stops shrinking)
  • ["expand"]: expand to an SXFunction
  • ["external", library, operation] or [..., opts]: a transform from a plugin library

Here opts accepts only "verbose"; passing the boolean task options raises an error. With "verbose": true the node and instruction counts after each pass are printed.

Parameters
fnameName of the result; defaults to the current name
passesExplicit list of passes
optsDict form: task booleans and "verbose"; passes form: "verbose" only

>>> z = MX.sym("z"); w = MX.sym("w")
>>> f = Function("f", [z, w], [sin(z)*cos(z) + sin(z)*cos(z)], ["z", "w"], ["r"])
>>> f, f.n_instructions()
(Function(f:(z,w)->(r) MXFunction), 9)
>>> g = f.transform()
>>> g, g.n_instructions()
(Function(f:(z,w[1x1,0nz])->(r) MXFunction), 6)
>>> f.transform([["simplify", "cse"]]).n_instructions()
6
>>> f.transform("fe", [["expand"]])
Function(fe:(z,w)->(r) SXFunction)
>>> try:
...   f.transform([["nope"]])
... except RuntimeError as e:
...   print("unknown pass verb" in str(e))
True


>> z = MX.sym('z'); w = MX.sym('w');
>> f = Function('f', {z, w}, {sin(z)*cos(z) + sin(z)*cos(z)}, {'z', 'w'}, {'r'});
>> f, f.n_instructions()
f:(z,w)->(r) MXFunction
9
>> g = f.transform(); disp(g), disp(g.n_instructions())
f:(z,w[1x1,0nz])->(r) MXFunction
6
>> f.transform({{'simplify', 'cse'}}).n_instructions()
6
>> f.transform('fe', {{'expand'}})
fe:(z,w)->(r) SXFunction
>> try, f.transform({{'nope'}}); catch e, disp(contains(e.message, 'unknown pass')); end
1


julia> z = ca.MX.sym("z"); w = ca.MX.sym("w");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [z, w], [sin(z)*cos(z) + sin(z)*cos(z)], ["z", "w"], ["r"]);
julia> println(f); ca.n_instructions(f)
f:(z,w)->(r) MXFunction

julia> g = ca.transform(f);
julia> println(g); ca.n_instructions(g)
f:(z,w[1x1,0nz])->(r) MXFunction
6
julia> ca.n_instructions(ca.transform(f, [["simplify", "cse"]]))

           ca.transform(f, [["nope"]])
       catch e

◆ transform() [4/4]

Function casadi::Function::transform ( const std::vector< std::vector< GenericType > > &  passes,
const Dict &  opts = Dict() 
) const
inherited

Only defined for SXFunction and MXFunction. The result has the same signature, names and options; only the expression graph differs.

The dict form runs a single "simplify" pass whose tasks are selected with boolean options, all defaulting to on except combine_terms:

  • empty_inputs: an input whose symbols do not occur in any output is replaced by an all-zero sparse matrix of the same shape (its nnz becomes 0)
  • cse: common subexpression elimination
  • ref_count: drop unreferenced nodes, repeated until a fixed point
  • const_folding: evaluate constant subexpressions
  • combine_terms: merge like terms of sums

Unknown keys raise an error.

The passes form runs an explicit list of passes in order. Each pass is a list whose first entry is the verb:

  • ["simplify", task, ...] with tasks from the list above; an integer before a task is its run count (N>0: N times, 0: until the graph stops shrinking)
  • ["expand"]: expand to an SXFunction
  • ["external", library, operation] or [..., opts]: a transform from a plugin library

Here opts accepts only "verbose"; passing the boolean task options raises an error. With "verbose": true the node and instruction counts after each pass are printed.

Parameters
fnameName of the result; defaults to the current name
passesExplicit list of passes
optsDict form: task booleans and "verbose"; passes form: "verbose" only

>>> try:

◆ uses_output()

virtual bool casadi::Callback::uses_output ( ) const
virtual

Derivative functions obtained from get_jacobian/get_forward/get_reverse receive the nominal outputs as extra inputs. When this returns false (default) those inputs are passed as empty (all-zero sparsity) placeholders; return true to receive the actual output values, e.g. to reuse them in the derivative computation.

>>> class Sq(Callback):
...   def __init__(self, name, opts={}):
...     Callback.__init__(self)
...     self.construct(name, opts)
...   def eval(self, arg):
...     return [arg[0]**2]
...   def has_jacobian(self): return True
...   def get_jacobian(self, name, inames, onames, opts):
...     x = SX.sym("x")
...     y = SX.sym("y", self.sparsity_out(0) if self.uses_output() else Sparsity(1, 1))
...     return Function(name, [x, y], [2*x], inames, onames, opts)
>>> class SqU(Sq):
...   def uses_output(self): return True
>>> Sq("f").jacobian().sparsity_in(1), SqU("f").jacobian().sparsity_in(1)
(Sparsity(1x1,0nz), Sparsity(1x1))


>> c = {'classdef SqJac < casadi.Callback', 'methods'};
>> c = [c, {'function self = SqJac(name)'}];
>> c = [c, {'self@casadi.Callback(); construct(self, name);', 'end'}];
>> c = [c, {'function r = eval(self, arg)', 'r = {arg{1}^2};', 'end'}];
>> c = [c, {'function b = has_jacobian(self)', 'b = true;', 'end'}];
>> c = [c, {'function J = get_jacobian(self, name, inames, onames, opts)'}];
>> c = [c, {'x = casadi.SX.sym(''x''); y = casadi.SX.sym(''y'', casadi.Sparsity(1, 1));'}];
>> c = [c, {'if self.uses_output(), y = casadi.SX.sym(''y'', self.sparsity_out(0)); end'}];
>> c = [c, {'J = casadi.Function(name, {x, y}, {2*x}, inames, onames, opts);'}];
>> c = [c, {'end', 'end', 'end'}];
>> d = tempname; mkdir(d); addpath(d); fid = fopen(fullfile(d, 'SqJac.m'), 'w');
>> fprintf(fid, '%s\n', c{:}); fclose(fid);
>> c = {'classdef SqJacU < SqJac', 'methods', 'function self = SqJacU(name)'};
>> c = [c, {'self@SqJac(name);', 'end', 'function b = uses_output(self)', 'b = true;'}];
>> c = [c, {'end', 'end', 'end'}]; fid = fopen(fullfile(d, 'SqJacU.m'), 'w');
>> fprintf(fid, '%s\n', c{:}); fclose(fid);
>> f = SqJac('f'); J = f.jacobian(); disp(J.sparsity_in(1))
1x1,0nz
>> g = SqJacU('g'); J = g.jacobian(); disp(J.sparsity_in(1))
1x1
>> rmpath(d); rmdir(d, 's');


julia> struct Sq <: ca.Callback end
julia> ca.eval_(::Sq, arg) = [arg[1]^2]
julia> ca.has_jacobian(::Sq) = true
julia> function ca.get_jacobian(::Sq, name, inames, onames, opts)
           x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y", ca.Sparsity(1, 1))
           ca.Function(name, [x, y], [2x], inames, onames, opts)
       end
julia> ca.sparsity_in(ca.jacobian(ca.callback(Sq(), "f")), 1)
Sparsity(1x1,0nz)

◆ which_depends()

std::vector<bool> casadi::Function::which_depends ( const std::string &  s_in,
const std::vector< std::string > &  s_out,
casadi_int  order = 1,
bool  tr = false 
) const
inherited

Returns one boolean per nonzero of input s_in: true when at least one of the outputs s_out depends on that entry (order 1) or depends on it nonlinearly (order 2). With tr true the relation is flipped: one boolean per nonzero of the vertically concatenated outputs, true when it depends on any entry of s_in. The analysis is structural (it follows the graph, not the values).

Parameters
s_inName of one input
s_outNames of the outputs to inspect
order1 (any dependence) or 2 (nonlinear dependence); other values raise
trTranspose the relation, see above

>>> x = SX.sym("x"); y = SX.sym("y", 2)
>>> f = Function("f", [x, y], [x*y[0], y[1]**2 + x, 3], ["x", "y"], ["a", "b", "c"])
>>> f.which_depends("y", ["a", "b"])
[True, True]
>>> f.which_depends("y", ["a", "b"], 2)
[False, True]
>>> f.which_depends("y", ["a", "b", "c"], 1, True)
[True, True, False]
>>> f.which_depends("x", ["a"], 2)
[False]


>> x = SX.sym('x'); y = SX.sym('y', 2);
>> f = Function('f', {x, y}, {x*y(1), y(2)^2 + x, 3}, {'x', 'y'}, {'a', 'b', 'c'});
>> f.which_depends('y', {'a', 'b'})
1 1
>> f.which_depends('y', {'a', 'b'}, 2)
0 1
>> f.which_depends('y', {'a', 'b', 'c'}, 1, true)
1 1 0
>> f.which_depends('x', {'a'}, 2)
0


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x"); y = ca.SX.sym("y", 2);
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x, y], [x*y[1], y[2]^2 + x, 3], ["x", "y"], ["a", "b", "c"]);
julia> ca.which_depends(f, "y", ["a", "b"])
2-element Vector{Bool}:
 1
 1
julia> ca.which_depends(f, "y", ["a", "b"], 2)
2-element Vector{Bool}:
 0
 1
julia> ca.which_depends(f, "y", ["a", "b", "c"], 1, true)
3-element Vector{Bool}:
 1
 1
 0
julia> ca.which_depends(f, "x", ["a"], 2)
1-element Vector{Bool}:
 0

See also
factory, jac_sparsity

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1vx

◆ wrap() [1/2]

Function casadi::Function::wrap ( ) const
inherited

The wrapper has the same signature and names, carries over the derivative options (ad_weight, ad_weight_sp, max_num_dir, is_diff_in/out, derivative_of) and is cached by name. Classes without their own derivative or slice implementation use it internally; it is also a way to give any function a plain MX graph.

Parameters
nameName of the wrapper; defaults to "wrap_" + name()

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [sin(x)], ["x"], ["y"])
>>> w = f.wrap()
>>> w, w.n_instructions(), w.get_function()
(Function(wrap_f:(x)->(y) MXFunction), 3, ['f'])
>>> f.wrap("g").name()
'g'


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {sin(x)}, {'x'}, {'y'});
>> w = f.wrap(); disp(w), disp(w.n_instructions()), disp(w.get_function())
wrap_f:(x)->(y) MXFunction
3
f
>> f.wrap('g').name()
g


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x], [sin(x)], ["x"], ["y"]);
julia> w = ca.wrap(f);
julia> println(w); ca.n_instructions(w), ca.get_function(w)
wrap_f:(x)->(y) MXFunction
(3, ["f"])
julia> ca.name(ca.wrap(f, "g"))
"g"

See also
wrap_as_needed, expand

Extra doc: https://github.com/casadi/casadi/wiki/L_1vv

◆ wrap_as_needed() [1/2]

Function casadi::Function::wrap_as_needed ( const Dict &  opts) const
inherited

Like wrap, but the wrapper is built with opts (merged with the derivative options of this function) and is not cached. The named overload returns this function itself when opts is empty and name equals its current name; the unnamed overload always wraps, under the name "wrap_" + name().

>>> x = SX.sym("x")
>>> f = Function("f", [x], [sin(x)])
>>> f.wrap_as_needed("f", {}).class_name(), f.wrap_as_needed({}).class_name()
('SXFunction', 'MXFunction')
>>> f.wrap_as_needed("g", {"ad_weight": 1}).name()
'g'


>> x = SX.sym('x');
>> f = Function('f', {x}, {sin(x)});
>> f.wrap_as_needed('f', struct()).class_name()
SXFunction
>> f.wrap_as_needed(struct()).class_name()
MXFunction
>> f.wrap_as_needed('g', struct('ad_weight', 1)).name()
g


julia> x = ca.SX.sym("x");
julia> f = ca.Function("f", [x], [sin(x)]);
julia> ca.class_name(ca.wrap_as_needed(f, "f", Dict()))
"SXFunction"
julia> ca.class_name(ca.wrap_as_needed(f, Dict()))
"MXFunction"
julia> ca.name(ca.wrap_as_needed(f, "g", Dict("ad_weight" => 1)))
"g"
